<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> What subunit assembly does this protein form?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> A homodimer<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A homohexamer arranged as a trimer of dimers<br><br><strong>C.</strong> A homotrimer<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A homotetramer<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>Analyzing the protein sequence (~197 amino acids), I can identify several key features:<br><br>**Coiled-coil analysis:** The N-terminal region (approximately residues 10-70) shows a characteristic pattern of hydrophobic residues spaced at heptad repeat intervals (abcdefg pattern), which is diagnostic of coiled-coil structures. The sequence &quot;IVDISSKDIVLREAVVEGYIKLRKETIEKIKNKEVEK&quot; displays the classic arrangement of hydrophobic residues at positions &#x27;a&#x27; and &#x27;d&#x27; of the heptad, consistent with a trimeric coiled-coil.<br><br>**Length considerations:** The coiled-coil region spans approximately 60 amino acids (~8 heptads), which is optimal for a trimeric coiled-coil formation. Trimeric coiled-coils typically require this length to provide sufficient hydrophobic burial and stability.<br><br>**Overall architecture:** The protein has a compact, modular architecture with a helical N-terminal domain (coiled-coil) and a mixed secondary structure C-terminal region.
